\Sigma_{i}\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
, i.e.

(4.9)

\Sigma_{i}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\\
\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}.

∎

Multi-additive functors are the categorical generalization of multi-additive group homomorphism, as Picard groupoids are the categorical generalization of abelian groups. For the precise definition of a multi-monoidal functors, readers can refer to
[
Duc05
]
§1.2.

For functorial properties, we discuss a little more on
\Sigma_{i}
. After we prove that our Deligne pairing is a symmetric multi-additive functor, we can see that
\Sigma_{i}
is a natural isomorphism of two functors.

Proposition 4.20
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Let
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant d
be two integers,
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}
be
f
-sufficiently ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and define

F(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i-1},\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}_{i+1},\ldots,\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{j-1},\mathcal{N},\mathcal{L}_{j+1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

for simplicity. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}

where
\Sigma_{i}
and
\Sigma_{j}
are defined by (
4.9
), and the left vertical arrow is the unique canonical isomorphism in the symmetric monoidal category—
\cPic(S)
.

Proof.

We can assume that
S
is integral, and let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Note that
\Sigma_{i}
and
\Sigma_{j}
are induced by
\Sigma_{i,\eta}
and
\Sigma_{j,\eta}
on the generic fiber
X_{\eta}\to\eta
respectively, and that
\Sigma_{i,\eta}
and
\Sigma_{j,\eta}
satisfy the corresponding commutative diagram on the generic fiber since they are natural isomorphisms of the multi-additive functor—Deligne pairings for flat morphisms.
∎

Proposition
4.20
can be viewed as
\Sigma_{j}\circ\Sigma_{i}=\Sigma_{i}\circ\Sigma_{j}
, which implies the order of operations of canceling parentheses (i.e. the order of
\Sigma_{i}
’s) for an expression
\langle\ldots\rangle_{X/S}
with
\otimes
-products is not important since there is only one isomorphism from the original expression to the final expression, regardless of the order.

Definition 4.21
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a projective morphism of noetherian schemes. Define

\Pic(X)_{f}=\{\mathcal{L}\in\Pic(X)\mid\text{there exist }\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\in\Pic(X)_{f\text{-s.a}}\\
\text{ such that }\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}^{-1}\}.

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Then we can associate any
d+1
invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\in\Pic(X)_{f}
with an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module, which we define to be
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
.

Proof.

For any integer
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
, suppose that
\mathcal{L}_{i}=\mathcal{L}_{i,0}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{i,1}^{-1}
, where
\mathcal{L}_{i,0},\mathcal{L}_{i,1}\in\Pic(X)_{f\text{-s.a}}
. Let
E
be the set of all the maps
h\colon\{1,2,\ldots,d+1\}\to\{0,1\}
. For
h\in E
, let
\epsilon_{h}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}(-1)^{h(i)}
. We define

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}=\bigotimes_{h\in E}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,h(1)},\mathcal{L}_{2,h(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,h(d+1)}\rangle^{\epsilon_{h}}_{X/S}.

By Proposition
4.19
and
4.20
,
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{L}_{i,0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{i,1}
. More precisely, for two different choices, there is a unique isomorphism between the two results.
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a projective morphism of noetherian schemes. Suppose that
S
is an affine scheme. Then
\Pic(X)=\Pic(X)_{f}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{O}_{X}(1)
be an
f
-very ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. For any
\mathcal{L}\in\Pic(X)
, by
[
GD61
]
Chapter 2 (4.6.8), we know that there exists a positive integer
n_{0}
such that for every integer
n\geqslant n_{0}
, the canonical morphisms

f^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}(n)\to\mathcal{O}_{X}(n)\quad\text{and}\quad f^{*}f_{*}\mathcal{L}(n)\to\mathcal{L}(n)

are both surjective. Let
\mathcal{L}_{0}=\mathcal{L}(n_{0})
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(n_{0})
. Then we see that
\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\in\Pic(X)_{f\text{-s.a}}
, and thus
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{1}^{-1}\in\Pic(X)_{f}
.
∎

Theorem 4.24
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Then we can associate any
d+1
invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}
with an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module, which we define to be
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
.

Proof.

Let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be an affine open cover of
S
, and let
f_{i}=f|_{U_{i}}\colon f^{-1}(U_{i})\to U_{i}
. For each
i\in I
and integer
1\leqslant j\leqslant d+1
,
\mathcal{L}_{j}\in\Pic(f^{-1}(U_{i}))_{f_{i}}
by Proposition
4.23
. Thus we can get an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{U_{i}}
-module
\mathcal{J}_{i}
by Theorem
4.22
. For
i,i^{\prime}\in I
, by Proposition
4.12
, we can find a canonical isomorphism

\theta_{i,i^{\prime}}\colon\mathcal{J}_{i}|_{U_{i}\cap U_{i^{\prime}}}\to\mathcal{J}_{i^{\prime}}|_{U_{i}\cap U_{i^{\prime}}}

such that
\theta_{ii}=\id
,
\theta_{ii^{\prime}}=\theta_{i^{\prime}i}^{-1}
, and for another
i^{\prime\prime}\in I
,
\theta_{i^{\prime}i^{\prime\prime}}\circ\theta_{ii^{\prime}}=\theta_{ii^{\prime\prime}}
on
U_{i}\cap U_{i^{\prime}}\cap U_{i^{\prime\prime}}
. Therefore, we can glue
\mathcal{J}_{i}
’s on the overlaps
U_{i}\cap U_{i^{\prime}}
to obtain an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module, which we define to be
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
.
∎

Definition 4.25
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Our
Deligne pairing
is defined to be

(4.10)

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\colon\Pic(X)^{d+1}\to\Pic(S).

4.4.
Symmetry and multi-additivity

The following three propositions are natural generalization of Proposition
4.15
,
4.16
,
4.19
, and
4.20
. Proposition
4.26
will be crucial for the proof of symmetry, muiti-additivity, and functorial properties of our Deligne pairing.

Proposition 4.26
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is integral and normal, and that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}
are
d+1
invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n+1}\rangle_{X/S,\,P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n+1}\rangle_{X/S,\,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n+1,P}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{2,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta},}

where
P\in S^{(1)}
, two vertical arrows are canonical isomorphisms, and two bottom objects are Deligne pairings defined by
[
Gar00
]
.

Theorem 4.27
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal,
f
is of finite Tor-dimension, and that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}
are
d+1
invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. Then our
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is identical to the corresponding Deligne pairing defined in
[
Gar00
]
.

Proposition 4.28
.

Our Deligne pairing (
4.10
) is multi-additive, i.e. we have a canonical isomorphism

(4.11)

\Sigma_{i}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\\
\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

for each integer
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
. Moreover, let

F(\mathcal{M},\mathcal{N})=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i-1},\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}_{i+1},\ldots,\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{j-1},\mathcal{N},\mathcal{L}_{j+1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},

and then we have the following commutative diagram

(4.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})\otimes F(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}_{j}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}\text{,}\Sigma_{j})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}_{j})\otimes F(\Sigma_{i}\text{,}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{j})}

Proof.

Directly by Proposition
4.19
, Proposition
4.20
, and the definition of our Deligne pairing.
∎

Proposition 4.29
.

Our Deligne pairing (
4.10
) is symmetric, i.e. we have a canonical isomorphism

(4.13)

\gamma_{\phi}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S}

for each
\phi\in S_{d+1}
, where
S_{d+1}
is the permutation group of
\{1,2,\ldots,d+1\}
. Moreover,

(4.14)

\gamma_{\psi\phi}=\gamma_{\phi}\circ\gamma_{\psi}

for
\phi,\psi\in S_{d+1}
,
\gamma_{\id}=\id
, and we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\ \ \Sigma_{i}\ \ }}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1)}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(1)}}\rangle_{X/S}\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\phi^{-1}(i)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi}\otimes\gamma_{\phi}}

(4.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xrightarrow{\ \ \Sigma_{i}\ \ }\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xrightarrow{\Sigma_{\phi^{-1}(i)}}\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi}}

where
\Sigma_{i}
and
\Sigma_{\phi^{-1}(i)}
are defined by (
4.11
).

Proof.

We can assume that
S
is integral, and let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Let

\mathcal{M}=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

and

\mathcal{N}=\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2)},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S}.

Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{\eta}&=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{2,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}\\
&=\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1),\eta},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2),\eta},,\ldots\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1),\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}\\
&=\mathcal{N}_{\eta}.\end{split}

We denote this isomorphism by
\gamma_{\phi,\eta}\colon\mathcal{M}_{\eta}\to\mathcal{N}_{\eta}
. For every
P\in S^{(1)}
, let
A=\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
, and then we have a canonical isomorphism

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{P}&=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,P}\rangle_{X_{P}/\Spec A}\\
&=\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1),P},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2),P},,\ldots\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1),P}\rangle_{X_{P}/\Spec A}\\
&=\mathcal{N}_{P}.\end{split}

We denote this isomorphism by
\gamma_{\phi,P}\colon\mathcal{M}_{P}\to\mathcal{N}_{P}
. Moreover, for every
P\in S^{(1)}
, by
[
Gar00
]
Theorem 4.2.7, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{\eta}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi,P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi,\eta}}

By Proposition
4.17
,
\gamma_{\phi,\eta}
restricts to a canonical isomorphism
\gamma_{\phi}\colon\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
, i.e.

\gamma_{\phi}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S}.

Note that
\Sigma_{i}
and
\gamma_{\phi}
are induced by
\Sigma_{i,\eta}
and
\gamma_{\phi,\eta}
on the generic fiber
X_{\eta}\to\eta
respectively, and that
\Sigma_{i,\eta}
and
\gamma_{\phi,\eta}
satisfy the corresponding properties and commutative diagram on the generic fiber since they are natural isomorphisms of Deligne pairings for flat morphisms.
∎

Diagram (
4.15
) can be viewed as
\Sigma\circ\iota=\iota\circ\Sigma
. Proposition
4.20
and
4.29
implies that the order of operations of canceling parentheses and interchanging factors (i.e. the order of
\Sigma_{i}
’s and
\gamma_{\phi}
’s) for an expression
\langle\ldots\rangle_{X/S}
with
\otimes
-products is not important since there is only one isomorphism from the original expression to the final expression, regardless of the order.

We summarize our results in this section as the following theorem.

Theorem 4.30
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Then (
4.10
) defines a symmetric multi-additive homomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\colon\Pic(X)^{d+1}\to\Pic(S).

5.
Functorial property

First we introduce a useful lemma.

Lemma 5.1
.

Suppose that we have the follow diagram in a category.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

Suppose that left and right faces of the prism are pull-back squares. Then there is a canonical arrow
E\to F
making the back face of the prism to be a pull-back square.

Proof.

Easy diagram-chasing.
∎

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Let
\mathcal{U}
be the open subset of
S
over which
f
is flat, and
\mathcal{V}
be the subset of
S
over which
f
is of finite Tor-dimension, i.e.

\mathcal{U}=\{s\in S\mid X\times\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,s}\to\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,s}\text{ is flat}\}

and

\mathcal{V}=\{s\in S\mid X\times\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,s}\to\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,s}\text{ is of finite Tor-dimension}\}.

Note that
\mathcal{U}\cup S^{(1)}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. Let
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism of noetherian normal schemes.

Definition 5.2
.

If
S
and
S^{\prime}
are both integral, then we say that
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
is a
good base change
if
g(\eta^{\prime})\in\mathcal{U}
and
g(S^{\prime(1)})\subseteq\mathcal{V}
, where
\eta^{\prime}
is the generic point of
S^{\prime}
. For general
S
and
S^{\prime}
, we say that
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
is a
good base change
if for each pair of irreducible components
C
of
S
and
C^{\prime}
of
S^{\prime}
such that
g(C^{\prime})\subseteq C
,
g|_{C^{\prime}}\colon C^{\prime}\to C
is a good base change.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be a good base change. Let
X
be a scheme and
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of pure relative dimension
d
. Let
X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
and
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
. Then we have a natural isomorphism

\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\to g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}.

Proof.

We can assume that both
S
and
S^{\prime}
are integral. Let
\eta^{\prime}
be the generic point of
S
,
Q=g(\eta^{\prime})\in\mathcal{U}
,
B=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,Q}
, and let
X_{B}=S\times_{S}\Spec B
. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S,}

where the front, left, and right faces of the prism are all pull-back squares. Then by Lemma
5.1
, there is a canonical arrow
X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}\to X_{B}
such that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec B}

is a pull-back square. Let
h\colon\Spec B\to S
. From the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}

we obtain a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle(g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S})_{\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(h^{*}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}))_{\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,B},\mathcal{L}_{2,B},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,B}\rangle_{X_{B}/\Spec B,\eta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1})_{\eta^{\prime}},(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2})_{\eta^{\prime}},\ldots,(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1})_{\eta^{\prime}}\rangle_{X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}/\eta^{\prime}}

(5.1)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},\eta^{\prime}}.

Let
P^{\prime}\in S^{\prime(1)}
and
P=g(P^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}
. Let
A=\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
,
A^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{S^{\prime},P^{\prime}}
,
X_{A}=X\times_{S}\Spec A
, and let
X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}=X^{\prime}\times_{S^{\prime}}\Spec A^{\prime}
. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}

where the front, left, and right faces of the prism are all pull-back squares. Then by Lemma
5.1
, there is a canonical arrow
X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}\to X_{A}
such that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A}

is a pull-back square. Then we have canonical isomorphisms

\displaystyle(g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S})_{P^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,P})

\displaystyle=g^{*}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,P}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A})

\displaystyle=\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1,P},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1,P}\rangle_{X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}/\Spec A^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\langle(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1})_{P^{\prime}},(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2})_{P^{\prime}},\ldots,(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1})_{P^{\prime}}\rangle_{X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}/\Spec A^{\prime}}

(5.2)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime},P^{\prime}}.

Since
P
lies in the Zariski closure of
\{Q\}
, we have the following commutative diagram

(5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}_{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S.}

Thus the isomorphisms (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) satisfy the commutative diagram in Lemma
4.17
. Then by Proposition
4.17
, we get an isomorphism

\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\to g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}.\qed

Diagram (
5.3
) is very useful, and will be used to deal with nearly all the issues concerning good base changes.

Theorem 5.4
.

All the canonical isomorphisms
\Sigma
’s (
4.20
) and
\gamma
’s (
4.29
) are compatible with any good base change
S^{\prime}\to S
.

Proof.

The morphisms are already there. For the compatibility, it suffices to check the diagrams over the generic point of each irreducible component of
S^{\prime}
, where the morphism becomes a flat morphism. Then the result follows from
[
Gar00
]
Theorem 4.2.6 and 4.2.7.
∎

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian scheme of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is integral and normal. Let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and let
\delta
be their intersection number on the generic fiber of
X\to S
. Let
\mathcal{M}
an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module. Then we have a natural isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S}\to\mathcal{M}^{\otimes\delta},

and the isomorphism is compatible with any good base change
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
, where
S^{\prime}
is also a noetherian integral normal scheme.

Proof.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\varphi_{\eta}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}\to\mathcal{M}_{\eta}^{\otimes\delta}.

Let
P\in S^{(1)}
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\varphi_{P}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,P}\to\mathcal{M}_{P}^{\otimes\delta}.

Moreover, for every
P\in S^{(1)}
, by
[
Gar00
]
Theorem 5.2.1.a, the isomorphisms
\varphi_{\eta}
and
\varphi_{P}
satisfy the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{P}^{\otimes\delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\eta}^{\otimes\delta}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\eta}}

By Proposition
4.17
,
\phi_{\eta}
restricts to a canonical isomorphism

\varphi\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,f^{*}\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S}\to\mathcal{M}^{(\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta})}.

Let
\eta^{\prime}
be the generic point of
S^{\prime}
,
Q=g(\eta^{\prime})\in\mathcal{U}
, and let
k
be the residue field of
Q
. Let
\delta_{k}
and
\delta^{\prime}
be the intersection number of
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}
over
k
and
\eta^{\prime}
respectively. By properties of flat families and flat base changes, we deduce that
\delta=\delta_{k}=\delta^{\prime}
.

For the compatibility with good base change, it suffices to check the diagram over the generic point
\eta^{\prime}
, where the morphism becomes a flat morphism. Then the result follows from
[
Gar00
]
Proposition 5.2.1.a.
∎

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
, and
g\colon Y\to X
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d^{\prime}
. Suppose that
X
and
S
are normal. Then we have a natural isomorphism

\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d},\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/S}\\
\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X}\rangle_{X/S},

and the isomorphism is compatible with any good base change
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
.

Proof.

We can assume that
S
is integral, and let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,\eta}\rangle_{Y_{\eta}/X_{\eta}}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta},\mathcal{M}_{d+1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,\eta}\rangle_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle(g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1})_{\eta},\ldots,(g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d})_{\eta},\mathcal{M}_{d+1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,\eta}\rangle_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}

(5.4)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d},\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/S,\eta},

where the third isomorphism follows from Theorem
5.3
.

For each
P\in S^{(1)}
, let
A=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
, and we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d},

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X}\rangle_{X/S,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,P},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X,P}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,P},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/X,P}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,P},\langle\mathcal{M}_{d+1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,P}\rangle_{Y_{A}/X_{A}}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d,P},\mathcal{M}_{d+1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,P}\rangle_{Y_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\langle(g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1})_{P},\ldots,(g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d})_{P},\mathcal{M}_{d+1,P},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1,P}\rangle_{Y_{A}/\Spec A}

(5.5)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d},\mathcal{M}_{d+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+d^{\prime}+1}\rangle_{Y/S,P},

where the third isomorphism follows from Theorem
5.3
. The isomorphisms (
5.4
) and (
5.5
) satisfy the commutative diagram in Proposition
4.17
. Then by Proposition
4.17
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{n},\mathcal{M}_{n+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{n+m+1}\rangle_{Y/S}\\
\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n},\langle\mathcal{M}_{n+1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{n+m+1}\rangle_{Y/X}\rangle_{X/S}.

For the compatibility with good base change
S^{\prime}\to S
, we can assume that
S^{\prime}
is integral, and then it suffices to check the diagram over
\eta^{\prime}\to\eta
, where
\eta^{\prime}
is the generic point of
S^{\prime}
. Let
X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
. Since both
X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}\to\eta^{\prime}
and
X_{\eta}\to\eta
are flat morphisms, the result follows from
[
Gar00
]
Proposition 5.2.3.b.
∎

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d\geqslant 1
, and
D
be an effective Cartier divisor on
X
such that
D\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-1
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Then we have a natural isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{D}\rangle_{D/S},

where we just delete
\mathcal{O}(D)
in the second expression. Moreover, this isomorphism is compatible with any good base change
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
.

Proof.

We can assume that
S
is integral, and let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. From the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\eta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

we deduce that for each
\mathcal{L}\in\Pic(X)
,
(\mathcal{L}|_{D})_{\eta}=\mathcal{L}_{\eta}|_{D_{\eta}}
, where
\mathcal{L}_{\eta}
is the abbreviation for
\mathcal{L}_{X_{\eta}}
. Since
D
is an effective Cartier divisor, the canonical section
s_{D}
of
\mathcal{O}(D)
is a regular global section. Then by
[
Gar00
]
Proposition 4.3.1, we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D),\dots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D)_{\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D_{\eta}),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta}|_{D_{\eta}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,\eta}|_{D_{\eta}}\rangle_{D_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle(\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D})_{\eta},\ldots,(\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{D})_{\eta}\rangle_{D_{\eta}/\eta}

(5.6)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{D}\rangle_{D/S,\eta}.

Let
P\in S^{(1)}
, and
A=\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
. From the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}

we deduce that for any
\mathcal{L}\in\Pic(X)
,
(\mathcal{L}|_{D})_{A}=\mathcal{L}_{A}|_{D_{A}}
, where
\mathcal{L}_{A}
is the abbreviation for
\mathcal{L}_{X_{A}}
. Then we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D),\dots,\mathcal{L}_{d}\rangle_{X/S,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,A},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D)_{A},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,A}\rangle_{X_{A}/A}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,A},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D_{A}),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,A}\rangle_{X_{A}/A}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,A}|_{D_{A}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d,A}|_{D_{A}}\rangle_{D_{A}/A}

\displaystyle=\langle(\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D})_{A},\ldots,(\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{D})_{A}\rangle_{D_{A}/A}

(5.7)

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}|_{D}\rangle_{D/S,P}.

Then isomorphisms (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) satisfy the commutative diagram in Proposition
5.3
. Then by Proposition
5.3
, we deduce the required isomorphism.

For the compatibility with good base change, we can assume that
S^{\prime}
is integral, and it suffices to check the diagram over
\eta^{\prime}\to\eta
, where
\eta^{\prime}
is the generic point of
S^{\prime}
. Let
X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
. Since both
X^{\prime}_{\eta^{\prime}}\to\eta^{\prime}
and
X_{\eta}\to\eta
are flat morphisms, the result follows from
[
Gar00
]
Proposition 4.3.1.
∎

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
, and
g\colon Y\to X
be a projective morphism. Suppose that
S
is normal,
Y\to S
is a surjective projective morphism of pure relative dimension
d
, and that there exists an open subset
U\subseteq X
which is dense in each fiber of
f
and satisfies that
g\colon g^{-1}(U)\to U
is an isomorphism. Then we have a natural isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S}.

Proof.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Note that
Y_{\eta}\to X_{\eta}
is a birational projective morphism. Then by
[
Gar00
]
Theorem 5.3.1, we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},\mathcal{L}_{2,\eta},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1,\eta},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2,\eta},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{Y_{\eta}/\eta}

(5.8)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S,\eta}.

For each
P\in S^{(1)}
, let
S=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
, and by
[
Gar00
]
Theorem 5.3.1 we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,P},\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,P}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1,P},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2,P},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1,P}\rangle_{Y_{A}/\Spec A}

(5.9)

\displaystyle=\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S,P}.

The isomorphisms (
5.8
) and (
5.9
) satisfy the commutative diagram in Proposition
4.17
. Then by Proposition
4.17
, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S}.\qed

6.
Divisor sequence and section sequence

Definition 6.1
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
, let
n\leqslant d
be a positive integer, and let
D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}
be effective Cartier divisors on
X
. Let
Z_{i}=D_{1}\cap\cdots\cap D_{i}
and let
Z_{0}=X
. We say that
D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}
is an
f
-regular sequence
if for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
,
Z_{i}
is an effective Cartier divisor on
Z_{i-1}
, and
Z_{i}\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-i
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Whether the sequence
D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}
is
f
-regular is independent of its order.

Proof.

For each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
s_{i}
be the canonical section of
\mathcal{O}(D_{i})
. Then
Z_{i}
is an effective Cartier divisor on
Z_{i-1}
is equivalent to that
s_{i}|_{Z_{i-1}}
is a regular section of
\mathcal{O}(D)|_{Z_{i-1}}
.

By
[
Gro65
]
(5.1.8), the dimension of each component of each fiber of
X\to S
decreases at most 1 after intersecting with each
D_{i}
. Thus
Z_{i}\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-i
for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
is equivalent to that
Z_{n}\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-n
.

Therefore,
D_{1},\ldots,D_{n}
is
f
-regular is equivalent to that
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
is regular, and that
D_{1}\cap\cdots\cap D_{n}\to S
of pure relative dimension
d-n
. The latter condition is independent of the order.
∎

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
, and suppose that
S
is normal. Let
n\leqslant d
, and let
D_{1},D_{2},\ldots,D_{n}
be an
f
-regular sequence of effective Cartier divisors on
X
. Let
Y=D_{1}\cap D_{2}\cap\cdots\cap D_{n}
. Then by Theorem
5.7
repeatedly, we get have an isomorphism

(6.1)

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D_{1}),\ldots,\mathcal{O}(D_{n}),\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1-n}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Y},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1-n}|_{Y}\rangle_{Y/S},

where we just delete
\mathcal{O}(D_{1}),\dots,\mathcal{O}(D_{n})
in the second expression.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assumptions as above. The isomorphism (
6.1
) is independent of the order of restrictions.

Proof.

we can assume that
S
integral, and then only need to verify the theorem over the generic point of
S
. Then we reduce to the flat case, and then the result follows from
[
EF24
]
Proposition 6.1.
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

Assumptions and notations as above. For each
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
, let
i_{j}
be the index of
\mathcal{O}(D_{j})
in the left side of (
6.1
), and let
\mathcal{L}_{i_{j}}=\mathcal{O}(D_{j})
. Let
\phi
be any permutation of
\{1,2\ldots,d+1\}
. Let
\sigma
be the corresponding permutation of
\{1,2,\dots,d+1-n\}
such that
\gamma_{\sigma}
sends
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Y},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{i_{n}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Y}\rangle_{Y/S}
to
\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)}|_{Y},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\phi(i_{1})},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\phi(i_{n})},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1)}|_{Y}\rangle_{Y/S}
. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i_{n}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Y},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{i_{1}},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{i_{n}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Y}\rangle_{Y/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(i_{1})},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(i_{n})},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1)}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)}|_{Y},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\phi(i_{1})},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{\phi(i_{n})},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{\phi(d+1)}|_{Y}\rangle_{Y/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\sigma}}

where the two horizontal arrows are the canonical isomorphisms defined as (
6.1
).

Proof.

We can assume that
S
integral, and then only need to verify the theorem over the generic point of
S
. Then we reduce to the flat case, and then the result follows from
[
EF24
]
Proposition 6.1.
∎

Definition 6.5
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
,
n\leqslant d
, let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
s_{i}
be a global section of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
. We say that
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
is an
f
-regular sequence
if
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
is a regular sequence, and
Z(s_{1})\cap\cdots\cap Z(s_{n})\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-n
.

Note that if
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
is an
f
-regular sequence
, then
Z(s_{1}),\ldots,Z(s_{n})
is an
f
-regular sequence
of effective Cartier divisors.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
,
n\leqslant d
, let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{n}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
, let
s_{i}
be a global section of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
. Suppose that
S
is normal, and that
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}
is an
f
-regular sequence. Let
Y=Z(s_{1})\cap Z(s_{2})\cap\cdots\cap Z(s_{n})
. Then by (
6.1
), we have a canonical isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{n+1},\mathcal{L}_{n+2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S},

which we denote by
[s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}]_{X/S}
. In particular, if
n=d
, then we have a canonical isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\textnormal{Nm}_{Y/S}(\mathcal{L}_{d+1}).

Moreover, let
s_{d+1}
be a global section of
\mathcal{L}_{d+1}
, then
[s_{1},\ldots,s_{d}]^{-1}_{X/S}(\textnormal{Nm}_{Y/S}(s_{d+1}))
is a global section of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
, and we denote it by

(6.2)

\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d},s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

Then Proposition
6.4
implies the following proposition.

Proposition 6.6
.

Assumptions as above. Let
\phi
be a permutation of the set
\{1,2,\ldots,d+1\}
such that
\phi(d+1)=d+1
. Then

\gamma_{\phi}(\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d},s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S})=\langle s_{\phi(1)},\ldots,s_{\phi(d)},s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
X
and
S
be quasi-projective over schemes a field of characteristic 0, and let
X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of pure relative dimension
d
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
, let
s_{i}
be a global section of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
. Suppose that
S
is normal, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant d
,
Z(s_{1})\cap Z(s_{2})\cdots\cap Z(s_{i})\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-i
. Then we can define a global section of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
, which we denote by
\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
. Moreover, if
\bigcap_{i=1}^{d+1}Z(s_{i})=\emptyset
, then
\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is a regular section of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. For
d=0
, the proposition is trivial. Now suppose that
d>0
, and we can assume that
S
is integral. Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{r}
be the irreducible components of
X
which dominates
S
, and we endow each
X_{i}
with the reduced structure. For each
X_{i}
, let
\delta(X_{i})
be its multiplicity in
X
. Let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
, and let
Z_{i}=Z(s_{1})\cap X_{i}
. Note that
s_{1}
is a regular section on each
X_{i}
. Then by
[
YZ24
]
Lemma 4.2.2, we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{X_{\eta}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{X_{\eta}}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{X_{i,\eta}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{X_{i,\eta}}\rangle_{X_{i,\eta}/\eta}^{\otimes\delta(X_{i})}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{i,\eta}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Z_{i,\eta}}\rangle_{Z_{i,\eta}/\eta}^{\otimes\delta(X_{i})}

By inductive hypothesis, for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
, we have a global section
\langle s_{2},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{Z_{i,\eta}/\eta}
of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{i,\eta}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Z_{i,\eta}}\rangle_{Z_{i,\eta}/\eta}
. Then by the above isomorphism, we get a section
s_{\eta}
of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,\eta}
.

For each codimension 1 point
P
, let
A=\Spec\mathcal{O}_{S,P}
. Then by
[
YZ24
]
Lemma 4.2.2, we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,P}

\displaystyle=\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{X_{A}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{X_{A}}\rangle_{X_{A}/\Spec A}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{X_{i,A}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{X_{i,A}}\rangle_{X_{i,A}/\Spec A}^{\otimes\delta(X_{i})}

\displaystyle=\otimes_{i=1}^{r}\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{i,A}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Z_{i,A}}\rangle_{Z_{i,A}/\Spec A}^{\otimes\delta(X_{i})}

By inductive hypothesis, for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
, we have a global section
\langle s_{2},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{Z_{i,A}/\Spec A}
of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{i,A}},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{Z_{i,A}}\rangle_{Z_{i,A}/\Spec A}
. Then by the above isomorphism, we get a section
s_{P}
of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S,P}
.

It is obvious that
s_{P}=s_{\eta}
on the generic fiber, and thus
s_{\eta}
is actually a global section of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
. We define
\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
to be this global section.

Moreover, if
\bigcap_{i=1}^{d+1}Z(s_{i})=\emptyset
, then for every
1\leqslant i\leqslant r
,
\langle s_{2},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{Z_{i,\eta}/\eta}
is regular. Therefore,
\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is also regular.
∎

7.
Deligne pairing of morphisms

7.1.
Flat case

In this subsection, we recall some base facts on Deligne pairing of morphisms in the flat case. Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective flat projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
.
[
Duc05
]
defines the Deligne pairing to be

(7.1)

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}=\bigotimes_{I\subseteq\{1,2,\ldots,d+1\}}\left(\det Rf_{*}\bigotimes_{i\in I}\mathcal{L}_{i}\right)^{(-1)^{d+1-\#I}},

where
Rf_{*}
is the derived complex.
[
Duc05
]
and
[
Dol22
]
prove the equivalence between this definition and
[
Elk89
]
’s definition. Moreover, we can see the equivalence from the generation and relation presentation given in
[
EF24
]
§6.1.2.

For each
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
, let
u_{i}\colon\mathcal{L}_{i}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{L}_{i}
be a isomorphism of invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. From the definition (
7.1
), we can easily get a morphism of invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules

\langle u_{1},\ldots,u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}.

Moreover, if
s_{1},\ldots,s_{d}
is an
f
-regular sequence,
then we have

\langle u_{1},\ldots,u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}(\langle s_{1},\ldots,s_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S})=\langle u_{1}(s_{1}),\ldots,u_{d+1}(s_{d+1})\rangle_{X/S}.

We have the following commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{\phi(1)},\mathcal{L}_{\phi(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\phi(1)},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\phi(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{{\phi(d+1)}}\rangle_{X/S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{1}{,}u_{2}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{\phi(1)}{,}u_{\phi(2)}{,}\ldots{,}u_{\phi(d+1)}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{\phi}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{i},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{M}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{M}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{M}_{i}\otimes\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{1}{,}\ldots u_{i}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle u_{1}{,}\ldots u^{\prime}_{i}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{1}{,}\ldots u_{i}\otimes u^{\prime}_{i}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma_{i}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{i},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{D},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}_{d+1}|_{D}\rangle_{D/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}|_{D},\ldots,\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\prime}_{i},\ldots\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}|_{{D}}\rangle_{{D}/S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{i}{]}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{1}{,}\ldots u_{i}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle u_{1}{,}\ldots{,}\hat{u}_{i}{,}\ldots{,}u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}u_{i}(s_{i}){]}_{X/S}}

where
\gamma_{\phi}
and
\Sigma_{i}
are the counterparts of (
4.13
) and (
4.11
) for Deligne pairing for flat morphisms,
u_{i}
is an isomorphism, and in the last diagram,
s_{i}
is an
f
-regular section of
\mathcal{L}_{i}
(so
u_{i}(s_{i})
is also an
f
-regular section), and
D=Z(s_{i})=Z(u_{i}(s_{i}))
.

7.2.
Equidimensional case

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is integral and normal, and let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. Let
f_{\eta}\colon X_{\eta}\to\eta
be the base change of
f
to
\eta
, and we see that
f_{\eta}
is a surjective flat projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
modules, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
, let
u_{i}\colon\mathcal{L}_{i}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}
be an isomorphism of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

For an isomorphism
u\colon\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
of invertible sheaves, we have
u^{-1}\colon\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{L}
, which induces an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}^{-1}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime-1}
. We denote this isomorphism by
u^{\otimes(-1)}
. Note that
u\otimes u^{\otimes(-1)}=u^{\otimes(-1)}\otimes u=\id
.

Theorem 7.1
.

There is a unique isomorphism

\varphi\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

such that

\varphi_{\eta}=\langle u_{1,\eta},u_{2,\eta},\ldots,u_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}.

Proof.

The uniqueness is obvious. For the existence, we can assume that
S
is affine.

Lemma 7.2
.

Suppose that for
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
,
\mathcal{L}_{i}=\mathcal{L}_{i,0}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{i,1}^{-1}
,
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i}=\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i,0}\otimes{\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i,1}}^{-1}
, and
u_{i}=u_{i,0}\otimes u_{i,1}^{\otimes(-1)}
, where both
u_{i,0}\colon\mathcal{L}_{i,0}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i,0}
and
u_{i,1}\colon\mathcal{L}_{i,1}\to\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{i,1}
are isomorphisms. Let
E
is the set of all the maps from
\{1,2,\ldots,d+1\}
to
\{0,1\}
. Suppose that for each
h\in E
, there is an isomorphism

\varphi_{h}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,h(1)},\mathcal{L}_{2,h(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,h(d+1)}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1,h(1)},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2,h(2)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1,h(d+1)}\rangle_{X/S}

such that

\varphi_{h,\eta}=\langle u_{1,h(1),\eta},u_{2,h(2),\eta},\ldots,u_{d+1,h(d+1),\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}.

Then there is an isomorphism

\varphi\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}

such that

\varphi_{\eta}=\langle u_{1,\eta},u_{2,\eta},\ldots,u_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}.

Proof.

For each
h\in E
, let
\epsilon_{h}=\prod_{i=1}^{d}(-1)^{h(i)}
. We know that

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}=\bigotimes_{h\in E}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1,h(1)},\mathcal{L}_{2,h(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1,h(d+1)}\rangle^{\epsilon_{h}}_{X/S}

and

\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}=\bigotimes_{h\in E}\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1,h(1)},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2,h(1)},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1,h(d+1)}\rangle^{\epsilon_{h}}_{X/S}.

Let
\varphi=\bigotimes_{h\in E}\varphi_{h}^{\otimes\epsilon_{h}}
. Then
\varphi
satisfies all requirements.
∎

Since we can find an ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
such that
\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\otimes\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{M},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2}\otimes\mathcal{M},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}\otimes\mathcal{M}
are all generated by global sections, by Lemma
7.2
, we can assume that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}
are all globally-generated invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules. By the same argument, we can assume that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}
are all
f
-very ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d\geqslant 1
. Suppose that
S
is an affine scheme. Let
\mathcal{L}
be an
f
-very ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. Then there exists an integer
n_{0}>0
such that for all integers
n\geqslant n_{0}
, there exists a regular global section
s
of
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}
such that
Z(s)\to S
is of pure relative dimension
d-1
.

Proof.

This is an easy application of
[
GLL15
]
Theorem 5.1.
∎

By apply Lemma
7.3
for
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}
in turn, we have find positive integer numbers
n_{1},n_{2},\ldots,n_{d}
such that for each map
h\in E
, the sequence
\mathcal{L}_{1}^{\otimes(n_{1}+h(1))},\linebreak\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\otimes(n_{2}+h(2))},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}^{\otimes(n_{d}+h(d))}
admits an
f
-regular sequence of global sections. Then by Lemma
7.3
, we can assume that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}
admits an
f
-regular sequence of global sections.

Now we prove Theorem
7.1
under the assumptions that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{2},\linebreak\ldots,\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{d+1}
are all
f
-very ample and that
\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d}
admits an
f
-regular sequence of global sections. Let
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}
be an
f
-regular sequence of global sections. Note that
u_{1}(s_{1}),u_{2}(s_{2}),\ldots,u_{d}(s_{d})
is also an
f
-regular sequence of global sections. Then define
\varphi
to be the morphism such that for every open subset
U\subseteq S
and every section
s_{d+1}\in\Gamma(U,\mathcal{L}_{d+1})
,
\varphi
sends
\langle s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d},s_{d+1}\rangle_{X_{U}/U}
to
\langle u_{1}(s_{1}),u_{2}(s_{2}),\ldots,u_{d}(s_{d}),u_{d+1}(s_{d+1})\rangle_{X_{U}/U}
. It is easy to see that
\varphi
is a well-defined isomorphism and that
\varphi_{\eta}=\langle u_{1,\eta},u_{2,\eta},\ldots,u_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}
.∎

We denote the isomorphism
\varphi
in Theorem
7.1
by
\langle u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
. Since
\langle u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is identical to
\langle u_{1,\eta},u_{2,\eta},\ldots,u_{d+1,\eta}\rangle_{X_{\eta}/\eta}
over the generic fiber, we see that
\langle u_{1},u_{2},\ldots,u_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
naturally satisfies the commutative diagrams listed at the end of §
7.1
.

By looking at the generic fibers, we see that
\Sigma
’s,
\gamma
’s, isomorphisms in Theorem
5.3
,
5.5
,
5.6
,
5.7
, and
5.8
are all compatible with isomorphisms in
\cPic(X)
, and they are also compatible with each other (see the precise statements in
[
EF24
]
Proposition 6.1, 6.10, and 6.13).

We summarize our results in this section as the following categorical version of Theorem
4.30
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Then our Deligne pairing defines a symmetric multi-additive functor

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\colon\cPic(X)^{d+1}\to\cPic(S).

And
\Sigma
’s,
\gamma
’s, isomorphisms in Theorem
5.3
,
5.5
,
5.6
,
5.7
, and
5.8
are all natural isomorphisms of functors.

8.
Deligne pairing of hermitian line bundles

In this section, we follow ideas from
[
Elk90
]
and
[
YZ24
]
to construction a canonical metric on Deligne pairing of hermitian line bundles for equidimensional morphisms.

Readers can refer to
[
Dem12
]
Chapter 2 §5.1 and §4.3 for the definitions of a complex analytic space
X
and its regular locus
X_{\textnormal{reg}}
. For concepts on complex analytic spaces, we follow the definitions in
[
YZ24
]
§2.1.1 and §4.2.2., and we recall some important concepts as follows.

Definition 8.1
.

Let
X
be a complex analytic space. A function
f\colon X\to\mathbb{C}
is called
smooth
if for each point
x\in X
, there is an open neighborhood
U
of
x
and an analytic map
U\to M
, where
M
is a complex manifold and
U
is embedded as a closed analytic subspace, such that
f
is the restriction of some smooth function on
M
.

Definition 8.2
.

Let
X
be a complex analytic space. A
smooth form

\alpha
on
X
is a smooth form
\alpha
on
X_{\textnormal{reg}}
such that for each point
x\in X
, there is an open neighborhood
U
of
x
and an analytic map
U\to M
, where
M
is a complex manifold and
U
is embedded as a closed analytic subspace, such that
\alpha|_{U_{\textnormal{reg}}}
is the restriction of some smooth form on
M
.

Let
X
be an integral quasi-projective scheme over
\mathbb{C}
, and let
L
be an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. For every point
x\in X
, let
k(x)
be the residue field of
x
, and let
L(x)=L\times_{X}\Spec k(x)
be the fiber over
x
, which is a 1-dimensional vector space over
k(x)
. A continuous (resp. smooth) metric on
L
is a families of metrics, one on each
L(x)
, such that for every open subset
U\subseteq X
and every section
s\in\Gamma(U,L)
(such
s
is called a
rational section
of
L
),
\|s(x)\|^{2}
is a continuous (resp. smooth) function on
U
. For any smooth metric
\|\cdot\|
on
L
, the Chern form, which locally can be written as

c_{1}(L,\|\cdot\|)=\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\|s\|,

where
s
is a rational section of
L
, is a (1,1)-smooth form on
X
.

Let
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})
and
(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})
be two invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules endowed with smooth metrics. Let
\varphi\colon\mathcal{L}_{1}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}
be an isomorphism of invertible sheaves. Then the norm of
\varphi
is defined to be

\|\varphi\|=\frac{\varphi^{*}\|\cdot\|_{2}}{\|\cdot\|_{1}}.

Note that
\|\varphi\|
is a nonzero smooth function on
X
. Locally, if
s
is a rational section of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, then

\|\varphi\|=\frac{\|\varphi(s)\|_{2}}{\|s\|_{1}}.

Therefore

c_{1}(L_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})=c_{1}(L_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})+\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\|\varphi\|.

For convenience, we generalize the conception of
f
-regular sequence for
f
of pure relative dimension 1.

Definition 8.3
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension 1, let
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and let
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
be global sections of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
respectively. Let
Y=Z(s_{1})\cap Z(s_{2})
. We say that
s_{1},s_{2}
is an
f
-regular sequence
if
s_{1},s_{2}
is a regular sequence, both
Y_{1}\to S
and
Y_{2}\to S
are finite, and
Y=\emptyset
.

Locally, if we identify both
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
, then
Y=\emptyset
means that
\mathcal{O}_{X}=(s_{1},s_{2})
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension 1, let
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
be invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, and let
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
be global sections of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
respectively. Suppose that
S
is normal and that
s_{1},s_{2}
is an
f
-regular sequence. Since
s_{1}|_{Z_{2}}
is regular,
s_{1}|_{Z_{2}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Z_{2}}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Z_{2}}
is injective. Since locally
\mathcal{O}_{X}=(s_{1},s_{2})
, we deduce that
s_{1}|_{Z_{2}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Z_{2}}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Z_{2}}
is surjective. Thus
s_{1}|_{Z_{2}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Z_{2}}\to\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Z_{2}}
is an isomorphism. By the same argument, we know that
s_{2}|_{Z_{1}}\colon\mathcal{O}_{Z_{1}}\to\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{1}}
is also an isomorphism. Then both
\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{2}/S}(s_{1}|_{Z_{2}})\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\to\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{2}/S}(\mathcal{L}_{1}|_{Z_{2}})
and
\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{1}/S}(s_{2}|_{Z_{1}})\colon\mathcal{O}_{S}\to\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{1}/S}(\mathcal{L}_{2}|_{Z_{1}})
are isomorphisms. Let
[s_{1}]_{Z_{2}/S}
and
[s_{2}]_{Z_{1}/S}
denote the inverses of
\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{2}/S}(s_{1}|_{Z_{2}})
and
\textnormal{Nm}_{Z_{1}/S}(s_{2}|_{Z_{1}})
respectively.

Proposition 8.4
.

Assumptions and notations as above. Then have the following commutative diagrams

(8.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{2}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{Y_{1}/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{1}{]}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{1}{]}_{Y_{2}/S}}

where
[s_{2}]_{X/S}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}
is the isomorphism in Theorem
5.7
for
D=Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
.

Proof.

we can assume that
S
integral, and then only need to verify the theorem over the generic point of
S
. Then we reduce to the flat case, and then the result follows from
[
Gar00
]
Proposition 4.3.1 and Theorem 4.2.7.
∎

The following is our main theorem regarding Deligne pairing of hermitian line bundles for equidimensional morphisms.

Theorem 8.5
.

Let
X
and
S
quasi-projective
\mathbb{C}
-schemes, and let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective
\mathbb{C}
-morphism of pure relative dimension
d
. Suppose that
S
is normal. Let
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1}),(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2}),\ldots,(\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\|\cdot\|_{d+1})
be invertible sheaves on
X
endowed with smooth metrics. Then we can endow
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
with a unique smooth metric
\|\cdot\|
such that

(1)

c_{1}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},\|\cdot\|)=\int_{X/S}\prod_{i=1}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i}).

(2) Let
d\geqslant 1
, and
s_{1}
be an
f
-regular section of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, then

(8.2)

\log\|[s_{1}]_{X/S}\|=-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i}).

(3) If
d=0
, then

\log\|\textnormal{Nm}_{X/S}(s)\|=\int_{X/S}\log\|s\|_{1}

for each nonzero rational section
s
of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
.

(4) The
\Sigma
’s and
\iota
’s defined in (
4.11
) and (
4.13
) are isometries.

(5) For each good base change (in the sense of Definition
5.2
)
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
, the isomorphism

(8.3)

\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\to g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}.

is an isometry, where
X^{\prime}=X\times_{S}S^{\prime}
,
g^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, and for each
1\leqslant i\leqslant d+1
, the metric on
g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{i}
is
g^{\prime*}\|\cdot\|_{i}
.

The symbol
\int_{X/S}
should be understood as follows. Let
n=\dim S
. If
\alpha
is a smooth
(n^{\prime},n^{\prime\prime})
-form on
S
. Then
\int_{X/S}\alpha
is the unique smooth
(n^{\prime}-d,n^{\prime\prime}-d)
-form on
X
such that for any smooth
(n+d-n^{\prime},n+d-n^{\prime\prime})
-form
\beta
with compact support on
S
, we have the following equation

\int_{X}\alpha f^{*}\beta=\int_{S}\left(\int_{X/S}\alpha\right)\beta.

Under suitable assumptions, the symbol
\int_{X/S}
can also be interpreted as the integral on fibers with multiplicities (refer to
[
YZ24
]
§4.2.2).

Proof.

We prove by induction on
d
. For
d=0
, the metric on
\textnormal{Nm}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L}_{1})
is uniquely determined by (3). We see that

c_{1}(\textnormal{Nm}_{X/S}(\mathcal{L}_{1}),\|\cdot\|)=\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\|\textnormal{Nm}_{X/S}(s)\|=\\
\int_{X/S}\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\|s\|_{1}=\int_{X/S}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1}).

Obviously, the additive isomorphism
\Sigma_{1}
is an isometry.

For each good base change
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
and each nonzero rational section
s
of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
,

\log\|\textnormal{Nm}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(g^{\prime*}s)\|_{S^{\prime}}=\int_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\log g^{\prime*}\|g^{\prime*}s\|_{1}\\
=\int_{X/S}\log\|s\|_{1}=\log\|\textnormal{Nm}_{X/S}(s)\|_{S},

thus
\|\cdot\|_{S^{\prime}}=g^{*}\|\cdot\|_{S}
.

Now suppose that
d>0
.

First we consider the case that
\mathcal{L}_{1}
admits an
f
-regular global section. Let
s_{1}
be an
f
-regular global section of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
, and let
Y_{1}=Z(s_{1})
. Then we have an isomorphism

[s_{1}]_{X/S}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S}

By inductive hypothesis, there is a unique smooth metric
\|\cdot\|_{Y_{1}}
on
\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S}
. Then we can endow
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
with a smooth metric such that
\|[s_{1}]_{X/S}\|
satisfies (
8.2
). By Poincaré–Lelong formula, we have an equation of currents

\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}=c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})-[Y_{1}].

By inductive hypothesis,

c_{1}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S},\|\cdot\|_{Y_{1}})=\int_{Y_{1}/S}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i}).

Thus

\displaystyle c_{1}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},\|\cdot\|)

\displaystyle=c_{1}(\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S},\|\cdot\|_{Y_{1}})-\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\|[s_{1}]_{X/S}\|

\displaystyle=\int_{Y_{1}/S}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i})+\int_{X/S}\frac{1}{\uppi\mathrm{i}}\partial\bar{\partial}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i})

\displaystyle=\int_{X/S}\prod_{i=1}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i}).

If we fix
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and vary
\|\cdot\|_{1},(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2}),(\mathcal{L}_{3},\|\cdot\|_{3}),\ldots,(\mathcal{L}_{d+1},\|\cdot\|_{d+1})
, then by the above construction, we can endow all Deligne pairings of the form
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
with a smooth metric. By inductive hypothesis, we see that
\Sigma_{i}\,(i>1)
and
\gamma_{\phi}
(
\phi
is a permutation of the set {2,3,…,d+1}) are all isometries.

Let
g\colon S^{\prime}\to S
be a good base change,
Y=Z(s_{1})
, and
Y^{\prime}=Z(g^{\prime*}s_{1})
. Then
g^{\prime*}s
is an
f^{\prime}
-regular section (
f^{\prime}\colon X^{\prime}\to S^{\prime}
) of
g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1}
, and
Y^{\prime}=g^{\prime*}(Y)
. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{1},g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}g^{\prime*}s_{1}{]}_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{*}{[}s_{1}{]}_{X/S}}

By inductive hypothesis, the bottom arrow is an isometry. Since

\log\|[g^{\prime*}s_{1}]_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\|=-\int_{X^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\log g^{\prime*}\|g^{\prime*}s_{1}\|_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(g^{\prime*}\mathcal{L}_{i},g^{\prime*}\|\cdot\|_{i})\\
=-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{d+1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i})=\log\|[s_{1}]_{X/S}\|,

we deduce that the top arrow is also an isometry.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
be global sections of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}_{2}
respectively, and suppose that
s_{1},s_{2}
is an
f
-regular sequence. Let
Y=Z(s_{1})
,
Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
, and
Y=Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}
. If we endow
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
with the metric we constructed above, then

(8.4)

\log\|[s_{2}]_{X/S}\|=-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}\prod_{i\neq 2}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i}),

where
[s_{2}]_{X/S}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}
is the isomorphism in Theorem
5.7
for
D=Z(s_{2})
. In particular,
\|[s_{2}]_{X/S}\|
is independent of the choice of
s_{1}
(though we do not know that whether
\|\cdot\|
depends on the choice of
s_{1}
at present).

Proof.

We have the following commutative diagram

(8.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{1}/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y/S}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{Y_{1}/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{1}{]}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{X/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{1}{]}_{Y_{2}/S}}

If
d>1
, (
8.5
) follows from Theorem
6.3
. If
d=1
, (
8.5
) is exactly (
8.1
). In this case, if we endow
\mathcal{O}_{S}
with the metric
\|\cdot\|_{S}
satisfying
\|1\|_{S}=1
, then we see that

\log\|[s_{1}]_{Y_{2}/S}\|=-\int_{Y_{2}/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\log\|[s_{2}]_{Y_{1}/S}\|=-\int_{Y_{1}/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}.

These notations are compatible with that in (
8.2
), which enables us to give a unified treatment for the cases
d>1
and
d=1
.

Let
\omega=\prod_{i>2}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{i},\|\cdot\|_{i})
. By
[
YZ24
]
Chapter 4 §4.2.2 Lemma 4.2.1,

\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})\omega-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})\omega\\
=\int_{Y_{2}/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\omega-\int_{Y_{1}/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}\omega.

Then

\displaystyle\log\|[s_{2}]_{X/S}\|

\displaystyle=\log\|[s_{1}]_{X/S}\|+\log\|[s_{2}]_{Y_{1}/S}\|-\|\log[s_{1}]_{Y_{2}/S}\|

\displaystyle=-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})\omega-\int_{Y_{1}/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}\omega

\displaystyle\phantom{=-\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{2},\|\cdot\|_{2})\omega-\int_{Y_{1}/S}}+\int_{Y_{2}/S}\log\|s_{1}\|_{1}\omega

\displaystyle=\int_{X/S}\log\|s_{2}\|_{2}c_{1}(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|_{1})\omega,

where the second equation follows from the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Let
f\colon X\to S
be a surjective projective morphism of noetherian schemes of pure relative dimension
d\geqslant 1
, let
\mathcal{L}_{1}
be an invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module, and let
s_{1}
and
s^{\prime}_{1}
be two
f
-regular global sections of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{2}
be an
f
-very ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module. Then for every point
t\in S
, there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
t
and an integer
n>0
such that there exists a regular section
s_{2}\in\Gamma(X_{U},\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\otimes n})
such that both
s_{1},s_{2}
and
s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2}
are
f_{U}
-regular sequences, where
f_{U}\colon X_{U}\to U
.

Proof.

Let
Y_{1}=Z(s_{1})
and
Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
. By shrinking
S
, we can assume that
S
is an affine scheme. If
d\geqslant 2
, then the results is an easy application of
[
GLL15
]
Theorem 5.1. So we can assume that
d=1
.

Let
\Ass(X)
be the finite set of all associated points of
X
, and similarly for
\Ass(Y_{1})
and
\Ass(Y^{\prime}_{1})
. Let
A=Y_{1,t}\cup Y_{2,t}\cup\Ass(X)\cup\Ass(Y_{1})\cup\Ass(Y^{\prime}_{1})
, where
Y_{1,t}=Y_{1}\cap f^{-1}(t)
and
Y_{2,t}=Y_{2}\cap f^{-1}(t)
. Note that
A
is also a finite set. Then by
[
GLL15
]
Theorem 5.1, we can find an integer
n>0
and a section
s_{2}\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{L}_{2})
such that
Z(s_{2})\cap A=\emptyset
. Let
Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
. It is obvious that both
s_{2}|_{Y_{1}}
and
s_{2}|_{Y^{\prime}_{1}}
are regular. It remains to show that we can find an open neighborhood of
t
such that
Y_{2,U}\to U
is of pure relative dimension
0
.

Let
f_{t}
denote the projective morphism
X_{t}\to t
, where
X_{t}=f^{-1}(t)
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{2,X_{t}}
is
f_{t}
-very ample, by
[
Gro65
]
Chapter 4 (5.3.1.3),
Y_{2,t}\neq\emptyset
. Then by
[
Gro65
]
(5.1.8),
\dim Y_{2,t}\geqslant 0
. Since
Y_{1,t}\cap Y_{2,t}=\emptyset
, by
[
Gro65
]
(5.1.8),
\dim Y_{2,t}<1
. Thus
\dim Y_{2,t}=0
. Therefore, by
[
Gro66
]
(13.1.5), there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
t
such that
Y_{2,U}\to U
is of pure relative dimension
0
.
∎

Now we prove that the metric we constructed on
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
is independent of the choice of the regular global section
s_{1}
. Let
s^{\prime}_{1}
be another regular global section
s^{\prime}_{1}
, and our goal is to show that the metric
\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
on
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
defined by
[s^{\prime}_{1}]_{X/S}
equals
\|\cdot\|
defined by
[s_{1}]_{X/S}
. Since this problem is local and
\Sigma_{2}
is an isometry, by Lemma
8.7
, we can assume that
\mathcal{L}_{2}
admits a global section
s_{2}
such that both
s_{1},s_{2}
and
s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2}
are
f
-regular. Let
Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
. Then we have the following isomorphism

[s_{2}]_{X/S}\colon\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}

Let
\|\cdot\|_{Y_{2}}
be the unique smooth metric on
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{3},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}
. Then by Lemma
8.6
,

\log\|\cdot\|=\log\|\cdot\|_{Y_{2}}-\log\|[s_{2}]_{X/S}\|=\log\|\cdot\|^{\prime}.

Thus
\|\cdot\|=\|\cdot\|^{\prime}
.

Now let
(\mathcal{L}_{1},\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{1})
be another invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules endowed with a smooth metric, Suppose that both
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}
admit an
f
-regular global section. Let
s_{1}
and
s^{\prime}_{1}
be
f
-regular global sections of
\mathcal{L}_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}
respectively. Note that
\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}
is endowed with a natural smooth metric
\|\cdot\|_{1}\otimes\|\cdot\|^{\prime}_{1}
, and that
s_{1}\otimes s^{\prime}_{1}
is an
f
-regular global section of
\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1}
. Thus we can endow each of
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
,
\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
, and
\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}
with a unique smooth metric by our above argument. Our claim is that the following isomorphism

\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\to\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},

which we denote by
\varphi
, is an isometry. The proof is as follows. By similar argument as the proof of Lemma
8.7
, we can prove the follow statement: if
\mathcal{L}_{2}
is an
f
-very ample invertible
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module, then for every point
t\in S
, there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
t
and an integer
n>0
such that there exists a regular section
s_{2}\in\Gamma(X_{U},\mathcal{L}_{2}^{\otimes n})
such that
(s_{1},s_{2})
,
(s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2})
, and
(s_{1}\otimes s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2})
are
f_{U}
-regular sequences, where
f_{U}\colon X_{U}\to U
. Using this statement, and the facts that this problem is local and that
\Sigma_{2}
is an isometry, we can assume that
\mathcal{L}_{2}
admits a regular global section
s_{2}
such that
(s_{1},s_{2})
,
(s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2})
, and
(s_{1}\otimes s^{\prime}_{1},s_{2})
are
f
-regular sequences. Let
Y_{2}=Z(s_{2})
. Then we have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{L}_{2},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}\otimes\langle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{Y_{2}/S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\langle\mathcal{L}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mathcal{L}_{d+1}\rangle_{X/S},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{X/S,\mathrm{i}}\otimes{[}s_{2}{]}_{X/S,\mathrm{ii}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}s_{2}{]}_{X/S,\mathrm{iii}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

where
